// lines indented to its text.
func (r *mdRenderer) list(n *ast.List, width int) []docLine {
        var marks []string
        for c, i := n.FirstChild(), 0; c != nil; c, i = c.NextSibling(), i+1 {
                if n.IsOrdered() {
                        marks = append(marks, fmt.Sprintf("%d.", n.Start+i))
                } else {
                        marks = append(marks, bullets[min(r.depth, len(bullets)-1)])
                }
        }
        markW := 0
        for _, m := range marks {
                markW = max(markW, ansi.StringWidth(m))
        }
        indent := markW + 1
        r.depth++
        defer func() { r.depth-- }()
        var out []docLine
        for c, i := n.FirstChild(), 0; c != nil; c, i = c.NextSibling(), i+1 {
                if i > 0 && !n.IsTight {
                        out = append(out, docLine{})
                }
                lines := r.blocks(c, max(width-indent, 1), n.IsTight)
                if len(lines) == 0 {
                        lines = []docLine{{}}
                }
                for j, l := range lines {
                        lead := strings.Repeat(" ", indent)
                        style := docPlain
                        if j == 0 {
                                lead, style = fmt.Sprintf("%*s ", markW, marks[i]), docDecoration
                        }
                        if len(l.segs) == 0 && j > 0 {
                                out = append(out, l) // an empty line stays empty
                                continue
                        }
                        l.segs = append([]docSeg{{lead, style, -1}}, l.segs...)
                        out = append(out, l)
                }
        }
        return out
}

// table renders t with its columns aligned between bars, the header bold
// and ruled off. When it does not fit, the widest column is narrowed and its
// cells wrapped, down to tableMinColumn; beyond that the lines are cut.
func (r *mdRenderer) table(t *east.Table, width int) []docLine {
        var rows [][][]docSeg
        for row := t.FirstChild(); row != nil; row = row.NextSibling() {
                style := docPlain
                if _, ok := row.(*east.TableHeader); ok {
                        style = docBold
                }
                var cells [][]docSeg
                for cell := row.FirstChild(); cell != nil; cell = cell.NextSibling() {
                        cells = append(cells, r.inline(cell, style))
                }
                rows = append(rows, cells)
        }
        if len(rows) == 0 {
                return nil
        }
        cols := len(t.Alignments)
        widths := make([]int, cols)
        for _, cells := range rows {
                for i, c := range cells {
                        if i < cols {
                                for _, l := range wrapSegs(c, 1<<20) {
                                        widths[i] = max(widths[i], segsWidth(l.segs))
                                }
                        }
                }
        }
        const gap = 3 // " │ "
        total := func() int {
                sum := gap * (cols - 1)
                for _, w := range widths {
                        sum += w
                }
                return sum
        }
        for total() > width {
                widest := 0
                for i, w := range widths {
                        if w > widths[widest] {
                                widest = i
                        }
                }
                if widths[widest] <= tableMinColumn {
                        break
                }
                widths[widest]--
        }
        bar := docSeg{" │ ", docDecoration, -1}
        var out []docLine
        for ri, cells := range rows {
                wrapped := make([][]docLine, cols)
                height := 1
                for i := range cols {
                        if i < len(cells) {
                                wrapped[i] = wrapSegs(cells[i], widths[i])
                        }
                        height = max(height, len(wrapped[i]))
                }
                for j := range height {
                        var segs []docSeg
                        for i := range cols {
                                if i > 0 {
                                        segs = append(segs, bar)
                                }
                                var cell []docSeg
                                if j < len(wrapped[i]) {
                                        cell = wrapped[i][j].segs
                                }
                                segs = append(segs, alignSegs(cell, widths[i], t.Alignments[i])...)
                        }
                        out = append(out, docLine{segs: trimSegs(segs)})
                }
                if ri == 0 {
                        var rule []string
                        for _, w := range widths {
                                rule = append(rule, strings.Repeat("─", w))
                        }
                        out = append(out, docLine{segs: []docSeg{{strings.Join(rule, "─┼─"), docDecoration, -1}}})
                }
        }
        return out
}

// tableMinColumn is the narrowest a column of a table is wrapped to.
const tableMinColumn = 10

// alignSegs pads segs to width as align says: right, centered or left.
func alignSegs(segs []docSeg, width int, align east.Alignment) []docSeg {
        pad := max(width-segsWidth(segs), 0)
        left := 0
        switch align {
        case east.AlignRight:
                left = pad
        case east.AlignCenter:
                left = pad / 2
        }
        out := []docSeg{{strings.Repeat(" ", left), docPlain, -1}}
        out = append(out, segs...)
        return append(out, docSeg{strings.Repeat(" ", pad-left), docPlain, -1})
}

// trimSegs drops the spaces at the end of a line.
func trimSegs(segs []docSeg) []docSeg {
        for len(segs) > 0 {
                last := &segs[len(segs)-1]
                last.text = strings.TrimRight(last.text, " ")
                if last.text != "" {
                        break
                }
                segs = segs[:len(segs)-1]
        }
        return segs
}

// anchor returns the name links give heading h after #, as GitHub makes it:
// lower case, spaces as -, without punctuation, numbered when it repeats.
func (r *mdRenderer) anchor(h *ast.Heading) string {
        var b strings.Builder
        for _, s := range r.inlineQuiet(h) {
                for _, c := range strings.ToLower(s.text) {
                        switch {
                        case c == ' ':
                                b.WriteRune('-')
                        case c == '-' || c == '_' || unicode.IsLetter(c) || unicode.IsDigit(c):
                                b.WriteRune(c)
                        }
                }
        }
        name := b.String()
        if r.anchors == nil {
                r.anchors = map[string]bool{}
        }
        for i := 1; r.anchors[name]; i++ {
                name = fmt.Sprintf("%s-%d", b.String(), i)
        }
        r.anchors[name] = true
        return name
}

// inlineQuiet returns the inline pieces of n without counting its links.
func (r *mdRenderer) inlineQuiet(n ast.Node) []docSeg {
        links := len(r.links)
        segs := r.inline(n, docPlain)
        r.links = r.links[:links]
        return segs
}

// inline returns the text of the inline children of n in style, as pieces;
// emphasis and code inside it take their own style, but in a heading or a
// link. A hard line break is a piece "\n".
func (r *mdRenderer) inline(n ast.Node, style docStyle) []docSeg {
        var out []docSeg
        r.inlineInto(n, style, -1, &out)
        return out
}

func (r *mdRenderer) inlineInto(n ast.Node, style docStyle, link int, out *[]docSeg) {
        inner := func(s docStyle) docStyle {
                if style == docHeading || style == docLink {
                        return style
                }
                return s
        }
        for c := n.FirstChild(); c != nil; c = c.NextSibling() {
                switch c := c.(type) {
                case *ast.Text:
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(string(c.Segment.Value(r.src))), style, link})
                        switch {
                        case c.HardLineBreak():
                                *out = append(*out, docSeg{"\n", style, link})
                        case c.SoftLineBreak():
                                *out = append(*out, docSeg{" ", style, link})
                        }
                case *ast.String:
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(string(c.Value)), style, link})
                case *ast.CodeSpan:
                        var b strings.Builder
                        for t := c.FirstChild(); t != nil; t = t.NextSibling() {
                                if t, ok := t.(*ast.Text); ok {
                                        b.Write(t.Segment.Value(r.src))
                                }
                        }
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(b.String()), inner(docCode), link})
                case *ast.Emphasis:
                        s := docItalic
                        if c.Level >= 2 {
                                s = docBold
                        }
                        r.inlineInto(c, inner(s), link, out)
                case *ast.Link:
                        id, before := len(r.links), len(*out)
                        r.links = append(r.links, string(c.Destination))
                        r.inlineInto(c, docLink, id, out)
                        if len(*out) == before { // only an image: a badge
                                r.links = r.links[:id]
                        }
                case *ast.AutoLink:
                        id := len(r.links)
                        r.links = append(r.links, string(c.URL(r.src)))
                        *out = append(*out, docSeg{printable(string(c.Label(r.src))), docLink, id})
                case *ast.Image, *ast.RawHTML:
                default:
                        r.inlineInto(c, style, link, out)
                }
        }
}

// segsWidth returns the width of segs in columns.
func segsWidth(segs []docSeg) int {
        w := 0
        for _, s := range segs {
                w += ansi.StringWidth(s.text)
        }
        return w
}

// wordToken is a word of inline text, which may be made of pieces in several
// styles, and the space before it, in the style of the text it was in.
type wordToken struct {
        space docSeg
        parts []docSeg
        brk   bool // a hard line break
}

// wrapSegs fills lines of width columns with the words of segs, breaking
// at spaces; a word wider than a line is broken where the line ends.
func wrapSegs(segs []docSeg, width int) []docLine {
        var words []wordToken
        cur := wordToken{}
        for _, s := range segs {
                if s.text == "\n" {
                        words = append(words, cur, wordToken{brk: true})
                        cur = wordToken{}
                        continue
                }
                for i, part := range strings.Split(s.text, " ") {
                        if i > 0 {
                                words = append(words, cur)
                                cur = wordToken{space: docSeg{" ", s.style, s.link}}
                        }
                        if part != "" {
                                cur.parts = append(cur.parts, docSeg{part, s.style, s.link})
                        }
                }
        }
        words = append(words, cur)

        var out []docLine
        var line []docSeg
        lineW := 0
        flush := func() {
                out = append(out, docLine{segs: line})
                line, lineW = nil, 0
        }
        for _, w := range words {
                if w.brk {
                        flush()
                        continue
                }
                if len(w.parts) == 0 {
                        continue
                }
                ww := segsWidth(w.parts)
                switch {
                case lineW == 0:
                case lineW+1+ww <= width:
                        line = append(line, w.space)
                        lineW++
                default:
                        flush()
                }
                for ww > width-lineW {
                        // Only a word too long for a line of its own is broken.
                        if lineW > 0 {
                                flush()
                                continue
                        }
                        line = sliceSegs(w.parts, 0, width)
                        w.parts = sliceSegs(w.parts, width, ww)
                        ww -= width
                        flush()
                }
                line = append(line, w.parts...)
                lineW += ww
        }
        if len(line) > 0 {
                flush()
        }
        return out
}

// sliceSegs returns the columns from to to of segs.
func sliceSegs(segs []docSeg, from, to int) []docSeg {
        var out []docSeg
        col := 0
        for _, s := range segs {
                w := ansi.StringWidth(s.text)
                if col+w > from && col < to {
                        s.text = ansi.Cut(s.text, max(from-col, 0), min(to-col, w))
                        out = append(out, s)
                }
                col += w
        }
        return out
}

// cutSegs returns segs cut to width columns, the last of them », when they
// are wider.
func cutSegs(segs []docSeg, width int) []docSeg {
        if segsWidth(segs) <= width {
                return segs
        }
        return append(sliceSegs(segs, 0, width-1), docSeg{"»", docOverflow, -1})
}

package ui

import (
        "fmt"
        "path/filepath"
        "strings"
        "unicode/utf8"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
)

// markJumpMsg asks the file selector to show mark letter in another file of
// the diff, at screen row screenRow.
type markJumpMsg struct {
        letter    string
        screenRow int
}

// isMarkLetter reports whether k names a mark: a letter a-z or A-Z.
func isMarkLetter(k string) bool {
        if utf8.RuneCountInString(k) != 1 {
                return false
        }
        c := k[0]
        return 'a' <= c && c <= 'z' || 'A' <= c && c <= 'Z'
}

// repoPath returns the path of file in marks: relative to the repository
// repo when inside it, else absolute.
func repoPath(repo, file string) string {
        abs, err := filepath.Abs(file)
        if err != nil {
                return file
        }
        if real, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(abs); err == nil {
                abs = real
        }
        if repo != "" {
                if rel, err := filepath.Rel(repo, abs); err == nil && filepath.IsLocal(rel) {
                        return filepath.ToSlash(rel)
                }
        }
        return abs
}

// setMark sets mark letter on the new-file line of displayed row v of d and
// returns the message to show.
func setMark(cfg *config.Config, d *fileDiff, letter string, v int) string {
        line := d.lineAt(right, v)
        if line < 0 {
                return "no line of the new file here"
        }
        m := config.Mark{Path: d.markPath, Line: line + 1, Text: strings.TrimSuffix(d.lines[right][line], "\r")}
        if err := cfg.SetMark(letter, m); err != nil {
                return "marks: " + err.Error()
        }
        return fmt.Sprintf("mark %s: %s:%d", letter, m.Path, m.Line)
}

// markLine returns the new-file line of mark m in d: its line when it still
// holds its text, else the nearest line that does, else its line number
// within the file, with a note. ok is false for an empty new file.
func (d *fileDiff) markLine(m config.Mark) (line int, note string, ok bool) {
        lines := d.lines[right]
        if len(lines) == 0 {
                return 0, "", false
        }
        want := m.Line - 1
        // Out from the old line until both directions left the file, which may
        // have shrunk far below it.
        for dist := 0; want-dist >= 0 || want+dist < len(lines); dist++ {
                for _, i := range []int{want - dist, want + dist} {
                        // Without the \r of a \r\n line end, which may have changed.
                        if i >= 0 && i < len(lines) && strings.TrimSuffix(lines[i], "\r") == strings.TrimSuffix(m.Text, "\r") {
                                return i, "", true
                        }
                }
        }
        return max(min(want, len(lines)-1), 0), "line changed", true
}

// reveal returns the cursor on line of side s of d in the screen lines of
// sh, unfolding the block of unchanged rows the line is in.
func (d *fileDiff) reveal(s side, line int, sh shape) spot {
        row := d.rowOf[s][line]
        for _, b := range d.blocks {
                if row >= b.start && row < b.end && !d.blockOpen(b) {
                        d.foldOpen[d.rows[b.start].Left] = true
                        d.buildView()
                        break
                }
        }
        return d.spotOf(s, line, sh)
}

// markMsg returns the message for having jumped to mark letter.
func markMsg(letter, note string) string {
        if note != "" {
                return "mark " + letter + ": " + note
        }
        return "mark " + letter
}

// notInDiff returns the message for mark letter m outside the diff shown.
func notInDiff(letter string, m config.Mark) string {
        return fmt.Sprintf("mark %s: %s is not in this diff", letter, m.Path)
}

// MarksPopup wraps the top-level model and shows the jump marks of the
// repository in a popup over it on \: enter jumps to the selected mark, d or
// del deletes it.
type MarksPopup struct {
        tea.Model
        cfg  *config.Config
        open bool
        listPopup
        msg string // a problem saving the marks
}

// WithMarks returns m with the marks popup.
func WithMarks(cfg *config.Config, m tea.Model) *MarksPopup { return &MarksPopup{Model: m, cfg: cfg} }

func (p *MarksPopup) Update(msg tea.Msg) (tea.Model, tea.Cmd) {
        switch msg := msg.(type) {
        case tea.WindowSizeMsg:
                p.width, p.height = msg.Width, msg.Height
                p.move(0)
        case tea.KeyPressMsg:
                if cmd, ok := popupKeys(msg, p.open, "marks", p.key); ok {
                        return p, cmd
                }
        }
        _, cmd := p.Model.Update(msg)
        return p, cmd
}

// key handles k in the popup, or opens it on \\.
func (p *MarksPopup) key(k string) tea.Cmd {
        if !p.open {
                p.open, p.cursor, p.top, p.msg = true, 0, 0, ""
                return nil
        }
        if isAction(k, "marks") {
                p.open = false
                return nil
        }
        marks := p.cfg.Marks()
        switch k {
        case "esc", "q":
                p.open = false
        case "ctrl+c":
                return tea.Quit
        case "down", "j":
                p.move(1)
        case "up", "k":
                p.move(-1)
        case "d", "delete":
                if p.cursor < len(marks) {
                        if err := p.cfg.DeleteMark(marks[p.cursor].Letter); err != nil {
                                p.msg = "marks: " + err.Error()
                        }
                        p.move(0)
                }
        case "enter":
                if p.cursor < len(marks) {
                        // Jump as the key of jump, ', and the letter do on the page.
                        p.open = false
                        p.Model.Update(runeKey(keyName(keyFile.row("jump").Keys[0])))
                        _, cmd := p.Model.Update(runeKey(marks[p.cursor].Letter))
                        return cmd
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// boxHeight is the height of the popup.
func (p *MarksPopup) boxHeight() int { return popupHeight(len(p.cfg.Marks()), p.height) }

func (p *MarksPopup) move(delta int) { p.moveIn(delta, len(p.cfg.Marks())) }

func (p *MarksPopup) View() tea.View { return tea.NewView(p.frame()) }

func (p *MarksPopup) frame() string {
        base := p.Model.View().Content
        if !p.open || p.width == 0 {
                return base
        }
        t := p.cfg.Theme
        marks := p.cfg.Marks()
        all := make([]string, len(marks))
        for i, mk := range marks {
                all[i] = fmt.Sprintf(" %s  %s:%d  %s ", mk.Letter, printable(mk.Path), mk.Line, printable(strings.TrimSpace(mk.Text)))
        }
        title := popupTitle(t, "marks", p.msg, "enter jump · d delete · esc close")
        return p.draw(t, base, title, all, "no marks: m and a letter sets one")
}

package ui

import (
        "fmt"
        "slices"
        "strings"
        "unicode/utf8"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
)

// switchFileMsg asks the file selector to show the next (1) or previous (-1)
// file.
type switchFileMsg int

// switchCommitMsg asks the log to show the next (1) or previous (-1) commit
// in history.
type switchCommitMsg int

// backMsg asks the log to return to the commit list, or from its flow view
// to the file list of the commit; or the file selector to its list when it
// shows the flow view.
type backMsg struct{}

// flowAtMsg asks the log to show its flow view on the file with this index
// of the commit whose files it shows: enter in that file list in flow mode.
type flowAtMsg int

// jumpMsg asks the file selector to show the line of the other end of moved
// code in another file: end, in the file of the group with index end.file,
// at screen row screenRow, with message msg.
type jumpMsg struct {
        end       moveEnd
        screenRow int
        msg       string
}

// refreshMsg asks the top-level view to load what it shows again: run git
// diff or git log again, list the directories again or read the two files.
// With quiet, as auto reload asks, it does not say when nothing changed.
type refreshMsg struct{ quiet bool }

func refreshAll() tea.Msg { return refreshMsg{} }

// nothingChanged is the message after loading again the same.
const nothingChanged = "nothing changed"

// askAutoReloadMsg asks DiffSwitch to toggle auto reload.
type askAutoReloadMsg struct{}

func askAutoReload() tea.Msg { return askAutoReloadMsg{} }

// showMsg asks the top-level view to show a message in its status bar.
type showMsg string

// refreshed returns the message after loading again: what was added,
// removed and changed, and the anchors dropped from changed files.
func refreshed(added, gone, changed, dropped int) string {
        var parts []string
        for _, c := range []struct {
                n    int
                what string
        }{{added, "new"}, {gone, "gone"}, {changed, "changed"}} {
                if c.n > 0 {
                        parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("%d %s", c.n, c.what))
                }
        }
        switch {
        case dropped == 1:
                parts = append(parts, "1 anchor dropped")
        case dropped > 1:
                parts = append(parts, fmt.Sprintf("%d anchors dropped", dropped))
        }
        if len(parts) == 0 {
                return nothingChanged
        }
        return strings.Join(parts, ", ")
}

// askDiffMsg asks DiffSwitch for the arguments of git diff to show next.
type askDiffMsg struct{}

func askDiff() tea.Msg { return askDiffMsg{} }

// askSettingsMsg asks SettingsPopup to open.
type askSettingsMsg struct{}

func askSettings() tea.Msg { return askSettingsMsg{} }

// toggleSettingMsg asks SettingsPopup to toggle the option of action, with the
// popup closed.
type toggleSettingMsg struct{ action string }

// settingKey returns the command toggling the option of action, nil when no
// option has it or, outside the log's flow view (logFlow), one that only
// matters there.
func settingKey(action string, logFlow bool) tea.Cmd {
        i := slices.IndexFunc(settingOptions, func(o settingOption) bool { return o.key != "" && o.action == action })
        if i < 0 || settingOptions[i].logFlow && !logFlow {
                return nil
        }
        return func() tea.Msg { return toggleSettingMsg{action} }
}

// pageKey returns the command of key h that all pages hand on: loading again,
// auto reload, the popups of git diff and the settings, or the option of its
// action, see settingKey; nil when h is none of them. The pages hand them on,
// and not the popups themselves as the help and the marks: o is a mark letter
// too, which m and ' take before the page looks at the key.
func pageKey(h keyHit, logFlow bool) tea.Cmd {
        switch h.action {
        case "reload":
                return refreshAll
        case "auto_reload":
                return askAutoReload
        case "git_diff":
                return askDiff
        case "settings":
                return askSettings
        }
        return settingKey(h.action, logFlow)
}

// settingChangedMsg tells the page below SettingsPopup that a toggle changed,
// so that it lays out what depends on it. The diff view does on any message.
// problem says why it is not saved, for the page to show when the popup is
// closed.
type settingChangedMsg struct{ action, problem string }

// toggleUntrackedMsg asks the top-level view to toggle the untracked files of
// its git diff, which only the file selector of one has.
type toggleUntrackedMsg struct{}

func toggleUntracked() tea.Msg { return toggleUntrackedMsg{} }

// untrackedOnlyInDiff is the answer of the other views.
const untrackedOnlyInDiff = "untracked files: only in jdiff diff"

// flowOnlyOfSeveral is the answer of the diff view of two files.
const flowOnlyOfSeveral = "flow: only with a file list or the log"

// toggleFlowMsg asks the log or the file selector to switch between the diff
// view of one file and the flow view of all files.
type toggleFlowMsg struct{}

func toggleFlow() tea.Msg { return toggleFlowMsg{} }

// firstLine returns the first line of err: git writes errors of several lines,
// where the status bar and notes have one.
func firstLine(err error) string {
        line, _, _ := strings.Cut(err.Error(), "\n")
        return line
}

// saveState writes the toggles to state.toml, returning a message when that
// fails, else "".
func saveState(cfg *config.Config) string {
        if err := cfg.SaveState(); err != nil {
                return "state: " + err.Error()
        }
        return ""
}

// The direction in history is decided here only: historyDir turns a key
// into it, historyOrder.step into a step through a list of commits.

// historyDir returns where key h of history goes: 1 to the next commit, for
// the first key of its row, as N and n, -1 to the previous one, as P and p.
func historyDir(h keyHit) int { return direction(h.i == 0) }

// historyOrder is the order of a list of commits, as git log lists them:
// the step from a commit to the next one in history, -1 when the newest
// comes first, as by default, 1 when the oldest does, with --reverse.
type historyOrder int

// orderOf returns the order of a list of commits, the oldest first or not.
func orderOf(oldestFirst bool) historyOrder {
        if oldestFirst {
                return 1
        }
        return -1
}

// step returns the step through the list to go in direction dir in history.
func (o historyOrder) step(dir int) int { return dir * int(o) }

// switchCommit returns the command asking the log for the commit key h goes
// to in history.
func switchCommit(h keyHit) tea.Cmd {
        dir := switchCommitMsg(historyDir(h))
        return func() tea.Msg { return dir }
}

// toggle flips setting, one of the toggles of cfg, and saves them, returning
// a message when that fails, else "".
func toggle(cfg *config.Config, setting *bool) string {
        *setting = !*setting
        return saveState(cfg)
}

// runeKey returns the key press typing the character s.
func runeKey(s string) tea.KeyPressMsg {
        r, _ := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(s)
        return tea.KeyPressMsg{Code: r, Text: s}
}

// motion returns how many rows the cursor moves on key h, page rows for a
// page and far to the first or last row, whether h pages, so that views of
// file content move the window along, and whether h moves the cursor at all.
func motion(h keyHit, page, far int) (delta int, paging, ok bool) {
        switch h.action {
        case "move":
                return direction(h.i == 0), false, true
        case "page":
                return direction(h.i == 1) * page, true, true
        case "ends":
                return direction(h.i == 1) * far, false, true
        }
        return 0, false, false
}

func direction(forward bool) int {
        if forward {
                return 1
        }
        return -1
}

// ends returns the message for having reached the last or first item.
func ends(forward bool, last, first string) string {
        if forward {
                return last
        }
        return first
}

// Package ui implements the interactive side-by-side diff view.
package ui

import (
        "slices"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/diff"
        "jdiff/internal/notify"
        "jdiff/internal/source"
)

// Model is the Bubble Tea model of the diff view: a single file view of a
// fileDiff, which other views may show and change too.
type Model struct {
        *fileDiff

        screen      []sline       // screen lines of the displayed rows
        screenAt    map[sline]int // screen line -> its index in screen
        screenShape shape         // what screen was built for
        // The displayed rows screen was built for, to find the cursor's line
        // again when another view or a reload changed them.
        screenChanges int
        screenReloads int
        screenView    []vrow
        screenRows    []diff.Row

        cursor int // index into view
        sub    int // line of the cursor row in the normal layout
        active side
        top    int // first screen line
        xoff   int // horizontal scroll in columns

        width, height int
        canBack       bool // shown from the file selector, which handles backspace
        commitNav     bool // shown from the log, which switches commits

        msg    string
        watch  *source.File    // the files on disk, reloaded when they change; nil when not watched
        notify *notify.Watcher // reports their changes, nil while they are checked every watch_interval
        prefix string          // "m" or "'" while waiting for the letter of a mark
}

// New returns a model comparing oldLines (left) with newLines (right).
func New(cfg *config.Config, oldName, newName string, oldLines, newLines []string) *Model {
        d := newFileDiff(cfg, [2]string{oldName, newName}, [2][]string{oldLines, newLines})
        d.markPath = newName
        (&moveGroup{}).add(d, d.names)
        return newModel(d)
}

// NewWatched returns a model comparing the sides of f, which it reloads
// when they change on disk (see watch_interval).
func NewWatched(cfg *config.Config, f source.File) *Model {
        m := New(cfg, f.OldFile, f.NewFile, f.Old, f.New)
        m.watch = &f
        m.markPath = repoPath(cfg.Repo(), f.NewFile)
        return m
}

// newModel returns the single file view of d.
func newModel(d *fileDiff) *Model {
        m := &Model{fileDiff: d}
        m.buildScreen()
        if slices.Equal(d.lines[left], d.lines[right]) {
                m.msg = "files are identical"
        }
        if w := d.cfg.Warning(); w != "" {
                m.msg = w
        }
        return m
}

// Init starts watching the files, when they are on disk.
func (m *Model) Init() tea.Cmd {
        if m.watch == nil {
                return nil
        }
        w, cmd, msg := startWatching(m.cfg, m, diskPaths(*m.watch))
        m.notify = w
        if msg != "" {
                m.msg = msg
        }
        return cmd
}

func (m *Model) Update(msg tea.Msg) (tea.Model, tea.Cmd) {
        m.syncLayout()
        switch msg := msg.(type) {
        case tea.WindowSizeMsg:
                m.keepScreenRow(func() {
                        m.width, m.height = msg.Width, msg.Height
                        m.syncLayout() // wrapped lines take other pieces
                })
        case tea.KeyPressMsg:
                return m, m.key(msg.String())
        case watchTickMsg:
                if msg.owner != nil && msg.owner != m {
                        return m, nil
                }
                if msg := refreshFile(m.watch, m.fileDiff, m.syncLayout); msg != "" {
                        m.msg = msg
                }
                return m, watchTick(m.cfg, m)
        case changedMsg:
                if msg.w != m.notify {
                        return m, nil
                }
                if msg := refreshFile(m.watch, m.fileDiff, m.syncLayout); msg != "" {
                        m.msg = msg
                }
                return m, waitChanges(m.notify)
        case refreshMsg:
                m.refresh(msg.quiet)
        case showMsg:
                m.msg = string(msg)
        case toggleUntrackedMsg:
                m.msg = untrackedOnlyInDiff
        case toggleFlowMsg:
                m.msg = flowOnlyOfSeveral
        case settingChangedMsg:
                if msg.problem != "" {
                        m.msg = msg.problem
                }
        case findMsg:
                m.findText(int(msg))
        }
        return m, nil
}

// stop stops watching the files: the view is replaced.
func (m *Model) stop() {
        if m.notify != nil {
                m.notify.Close()
        }
}

// refresh reads both files again, whether their stamps changed or not. With
// quiet, it does not say when nothing changed.
func (m *Model) refresh(quiet bool) {
        if m.watch == nil {
                m.msg = "nothing to load again"
                return
        }
        m.watch.OldStamp, m.watch.NewStamp = source.Stamp{}, source.Stamp{}
        switch msg := refreshFile(m.watch, m.fileDiff, m.syncLayout); {
        case msg != "":
                m.msg = msg
        case !quiet:
                m.msg = nothingChanged
        }
}

func (m *Model) key(k string) tea.Cmd {
        m.msg = ""
        if p := m.prefix; p != "" {
                m.prefix = ""
                switch {
                case !isMarkLetter(k):
                case p == "set_mark":
                        m.msg = setMark(m.cfg, m.fileDiff, k, m.cursor)
                default:
                        return m.gotoMark(k, m.pos()-m.top)
                }
                return nil
        }
        h := keyFile.key(diffView, k)
        if m.replace != nil && h.action != "anchor" && h.action != "cancel_mark" {
                m.replace = nil
        }

        page := max(m.bodyHeight()-1, 1)
        if delta, paging, ok := motion(h, page, len(m.screen)); ok {
                if paging {
                        m.scroll(delta)
                } else {
                        m.moveCursor(delta)
                }
                return nil
        }
        switch h.action {
        case "quit":
                return tea.Quit
        case "pane":
                m.active = m.active.other()
        case "half_page":
                m.scroll(direction(h.i == 1) * (page / 2))
        case "scroll":
                if h.i == 0 {
                        m.xoff = max(m.xoff-8, 0)
                } else {
                        m.xoff += 8
                }
        case "change":
                m.nextChange(direction(h.i == 0))
        case "next_file":
                if m.canBack {
                        dir := switchFileMsg(direction(h.i == 0))
                        return func() tea.Msg { return dir }
                }
        case "history":
                if m.commitNav {
                        return switchCommit(h)
                }
        case "search":
                return askSearch
        case "match":
                m.findText(direction(h.i == 0))
        case "flow":
                if m.canBack {
                        return toggleFlow
                }
        default:
                return pageKey(h, false)
        case "anchor", "cancel_mark", "fold", "fold_all":
                m.act(h.action)
        case "moved":
                return m.jump()
        case "set_mark":
                m.prefix, m.msg = h.action, "set mark: press a letter"
        case "jump":
                m.prefix, m.msg = h.action, "jump to mark: press its letter"
        }
        return nil
}

// gotoMark moves the cursor to the line of mark letter, at screen row
// screenRow. A mark in another file of the diff is shown by the file
// selector.
func (m *Model) gotoMark(letter string, screenRow int) tea.Cmd {
        mk, ok := m.cfg.Mark(letter)
        switch {
        case !ok:
                m.msg = "no mark " + letter
        case mk.Path == m.markPath:
                if at, ok := m.markSpot(letter, mk); ok {
                        m.jumpTo(at)
                }
        case m.canBack:
                j := markJumpMsg{letter, screenRow}
                return func() tea.Msg { return j }
        default:
                m.msg = notInDiff(letter, mk)
        }
        return nil
}

// markSpot returns the cursor on the line of mark mk, called letter, of
// this file, unfolding it, and sets the message. ok is false for an empty
// new file.
func (m *Model) markSpot(letter string, mk config.Mark) (at spot, ok bool) {
        line, note, ok := m.markLine(mk)
        if !ok {
                m.msg = markMsg(letter, "the new file is empty")
                return spot{}, false
        }
        m.msg = markMsg(letter, note)
        return m.reveal(right, line, m.shape()), true
}

// jump moves the cursor to the other end of the moved line under it (see
// jumpTo). An end in another file is shown by the file selector, at the
// cursor's screen row.
func (m *Model) jump() tea.Cmd {
        end, msg, ok := m.otherEnd(spot{m.cursor, m.sub, m.active}, m.screenShape)
        if !ok {
                m.msg = msg
                return nil
        }
        if end.file != m.groupIndex {
                j := jumpMsg{end, m.pos() - m.top, msg}
                return func() tea.Msg { return j }
        }
        m.jumpTo(m.spotOf(end.side, end.line, m.screenShape))
        m.msg = msg
        return nil
}

// jumpTo puts the cursor on at, for a jump within this view (g, '): the
// screen stays when at is on it, see jumpTop.
func (m *Model) jumpTo(at spot) {
        m.syncLayout() // a fold opened for at: the cursor keeps its line
        from := m.pos()
        m.active, m.cursor, m.sub = at.side, at.v, at.sub
        m.top = jumpTop(from, m.pos(), m.top, m.bodyHeight(), len(m.screen))
}

// show puts the cursor on at, at screen row screenRow.
func (m *Model) show(at spot, screenRow int) {
        m.active = at.side
        m.place(at.v, at.sub, screenRow)
}

// act runs an anchor or fold action on the diff at the cursor, which stays on
// its screen row.
func (m *Model) act(action string) {
        var msg string
        m.keepScreenRow(func() {
                changes := m.changes
                var at spot
                at, msg = m.fileDiff.act(action, spot{m.cursor, m.sub, m.active}, m.screenShape)
                if m.changes != changes {
                        m.buildScreen()
                }
                m.cursor, m.sub, m.active = at.v, at.sub, at.side
        })
        if msg != "" {
                m.msg = msg
        }
}

// realign recomputes the diff, after its settings changed.
func (m *Model) realign() {
        m.align()
        m.buildScreen()
        m.cursor = min(m.cursor, max(len(m.view)-1, 0))
}

// shape returns the shape of the screen lines for the current layout and
// width.
func (m *Model) shape() shape { return newShape(m.cfg, m.width) }

// buildScreen computes the screen lines of the displayed rows for the
// current layout and width.
func (m *Model) buildScreen() {
        m.screenShape = m.shape()
        m.screen, m.screenAt = m.screenLines(m.screenShape)
        m.screenChanges, m.screenReloads, m.screenView, m.screenRows = m.changes, m.reloads, m.view, m.rows
}

// syncLayout rebuilds the screen lines when another view or a reload changed
// the diff, keeping the cursor on its line, or when the layout was switched,
// here or in another view, or wrapped lines need other pieces, keeping the
// cursor on its line, piece and screen row.
func (m *Model) syncLayout() {
        switch {
        case m.screenChanges != m.changes:
                m.cursor = m.cursorAfterChange()
                m.buildScreen()
                m.scrollToCursor()
        case m.screenShape != m.shape():
                from := m.screenShape
                m.keepScreenRow(func() {
                        m.buildScreen()
                        if m.cursor < len(m.view) {
                                m.sub = m.convertSub(m.cursor, m.sub, from, m.screenShape, m.active)
                        }
                })
        }
}

// cursorAfterChange returns the displayed row that shows the line of the
// cursor's row in the rows screen was built for: its old line, else its new
// one, followed through a reload since.
func (m *Model) cursorAfterChange() int {
        if m.cursor >= len(m.screenView) || len(m.view) == 0 {
                return 0
        }
        if v := m.follow(m.screenRows[m.screenView[m.cursor].row], m.screenReloads != m.reloads); v >= 0 {
                return v
        }
        return min(m.cursor, len(m.view)-1)
}

// pos returns the screen line of the cursor.
func (m *Model) pos() int {
        if m.cursor >= len(m.view) {
                return 0
        }
        return m.screenAt[sline{m.cursor, min(m.sub, m.rowLines(m.cursor, m.screenShape)-1)}]
}

// place puts the cursor on line sub of displayed row v at screen row
// screenRow.
func (m *Model) place(v, sub, screenRow int) {
        m.syncLayout()
        m.cursor, m.sub = v, sub
        m.top = m.pos() - screenRow
        m.scrollToCursor()
}

// takeOver continues where flow f leaves this file: with its pane and
// scrolling, and, unless line is -1, its cursor on diff row line at screen
// row screenRow.
func (m *Model) takeOver(f *Flow, line, screenRow int) {
        m.active, m.xoff = f.active, f.xoff
        if line >= 0 {
                m.place(line, f.cursorSub(), screenRow)
        }
}

// moveCursor moves the cursor by delta screen lines.
func (m *Model) moveCursor(delta int) {
        if len(m.screen) == 0 {
                return
        }
        s := m.screen[max(min(m.pos()+delta, len(m.screen)-1), 0)]
        m.cursor, m.sub = s.v, s.sub
        m.scrollToCursor()
}

// scroll moves the window by delta screen lines, as far as it can, and the
// cursor along, so that the cursor keeps its screen row.
func (m *Model) scroll(delta int) {
        if len(m.screen) == 0 {
                return
        }
        top := m.top
        m.top = max(min(m.top+delta, len(m.screen)-m.bodyHeight()), 0)
        m.moveCursor(m.top - top)
}

// nextChange moves the cursor to the first line of the next (dir > 0) or
// previous block of changed rows.
func (m *Model) nextChange(dir int) {
        for i := m.pos() + dir; i >= 0 && i < len(m.screen); i += dir {
                if m.blockStart(m.screen, i) {
                        m.cursor, m.sub = m.screen[i].v, m.screen[i].sub
                        m.scrollToCursor()
                        return
                }
        }
        m.msg = "no more changes"
}

func (m *Model) bodyHeight() int { return max(m.height-2, 1) }

// keepScreenRow runs f, which may rebuild the view, move the cursor or
// resize, and then scrolls so that the cursor stays on the same screen row.
// Near the start or end of the file it moves as little as possible.
func (m *Model) keepScreenRow(f func()) {
        offset := m.pos() - m.top
        f()
        m.top = m.pos() - offset
        m.scrollToCursor()
}

func (m *Model) scrollToCursor() {
        m.top = scrollWindow(m.pos(), m.top, m.bodyHeight(), len(m.screen))
}

package ui

import (
        "fmt"

        "jdiff/internal/diff"
)

// moveGroup is the files searched together for moved code: the files of one
// commit or of a comparison of two directories, or the one file of a
// comparison of two files. Never files of different commits.
type moveGroup struct {
        diffs  []*fileDiff
        names  [][2]string // the names of the sides of the files, for messages
        aligns []int       // the alignments the moves were found for
        moves  diff.Moves
}

// add adds d, whose sides are called names, to the group.
func (g *moveGroup) add(d *fileDiff, names [2]string) {
        d.group, d.groupIndex = g, len(g.diffs)
        g.diffs = append(g.diffs, d)
        g.names = append(g.names, names)
        g.aligns = nil
}

// ensure finds the moves again when a diff of the group was aligned anew,
// after an anchor was added or removed or the file was reloaded.
func (g *moveGroup) ensure() {
        fresh := len(g.aligns) == len(g.diffs)
        for i, d := range g.diffs {
                fresh = fresh && g.aligns[i] == d.aligns
        }
        if fresh {
                return
        }
        in := make([]diff.MoveInput, len(g.diffs))
        g.aligns = make([]int, len(g.diffs))
        for i, d := range g.diffs {
                in[i] = diff.MoveInput{Old: d.lines[left], New: d.lines[right], Rows: d.rows}
                g.aligns[i] = d.aligns
        }
        g.moves = diff.FindMoves(in)
}

// movedTo returns where line of side s of d moved: for a removed line the
// line it moved to, for an added line the line it came from.
func (d *fileDiff) movedTo(s side, line int) (diff.Ref, bool) {
        if d.group == nil || line < 0 {
                return diff.Ref{}, false
        }
        d.group.ensure()
        moves := d.group.moves.To
        if s == left {
                moves = d.group.moves.From
        }
        r, ok := moves[d.groupIndex][line]
        return r, ok
}

// moveEnd is the other end of a moved line: line of side side of the file
// at index file of the group.
type moveEnd struct {
        file int
        side side
        line int
}

// otherEnd returns the other end of the moved line under the cursor at, in
// the screen lines of sh, and the message to show there, which says where
// the cursor came from: side by side the line of the active pane, else the
// one beside it. ok is false when neither moved.
func (d *fileDiff) otherEnd(at spot, sh shape) (end moveEnd, msg string, ok bool) {
        s := at.side
        if sh.unified {
                s = d.unifiedSide(at, sh)
        }
        line := d.lineAt(s, at.v)
        r, ok := d.movedTo(s, line)
        if !ok && !sh.unified {
                // Side by side the pane is for anchors: a moved line has no line
                // beside it, so the cursor may be in either pane.
                s = s.other()
                line = d.lineAt(s, at.v)
                r, ok = d.movedTo(s, line)
        }
        if !ok {
                return moveEnd{}, "not moved", false
        }
        how := "moved from"
        if s == right {
                how = "moved to"
        }
        return moveEnd{r.File, s.other(), r.Line}, fmt.Sprintf("%s %s:%d", how, d.group.names[d.groupIndex][s], line+1), true
}

// spotOf returns the cursor on the start of line of side s of d in the
// screen lines of sh (see firstSub).
func (d *fileDiff) spotOf(s side, line int, sh shape) spot {
        v := d.viewOf[d.rowOf[s][line]]
        return spot{v, d.firstSub(v, s, sh), s}
}

package ui

import (
        "cmp"
        "fmt"
        "regexp"
        "slices"
        "strings"
        "sync"
        "unicode"
        "unicode/utf8"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
        "jdiff/internal/diff"
        "jdiff/internal/settings"
)

// askSearchMsg asks SearchBar for the query to search for.
type askSearchMsg struct{}

func askSearch() tea.Msg { return askSearchMsg{} }

// findMsg asks the view shown to search for the query of config.Search from
// its cursor on, forward (1) or backward (-1).
type findMsg int

// matcher finds a query in lines. Case is ignored unless the query holds
// an upper case letter, also in a regular expression.
type matcher struct {
        q  config.Query
        re *regexp.Regexp // for a regular expression
}

// compile returns the matcher of q, or why its regular expression does not
// compile.
func compile(q config.Query) (*matcher, error) {
        m := &matcher{q: q}
        if q.Regex {
                src := q.Text
                if !strings.ContainsFunc(src, unicode.IsUpper) {
                        src = "(?i)" + src
                }
                re, err := regexp.Compile(src)
                if err != nil {
                        return nil, err
                }
                m.re = re
        }
        return m, nil
}

// compiled is the matcher used last: lines are drawn one at a time, all
// with the same query.
var compiled struct {
        sync.Mutex
        q config.Query
        m *matcher
}

// matcherOf returns the matcher of q, nil when q is empty or its regular
// expression does not compile.
func matcherOf(q config.Query) *matcher {
        if q.Text == "" {
                return nil
        }
        compiled.Lock()
        defer compiled.Unlock()
        if compiled.m == nil || compiled.q != q {
                compiled.q = q
                compiled.m, _ = compile(q)
        }
        return compiled.m
}

// spans returns where m's query is found in text, without overlaps; empty
// matches of a regular expression are left out.
func (m *matcher) spans(text string) []diff.Span {
        var out []diff.Span
        if m.re != nil {
                for _, loc := range m.re.FindAllStringIndex(text, -1) {
                        if loc[1] > loc[0] {
                                out = append(out, diff.Span{Start: loc[0], End: loc[1]})
                        }
                }
                return out
        }
        q := m.q.Text
        exact := strings.ContainsFunc(q, unicode.IsUpper)
        for i := 0; i+len(q) <= len(text); {
                part := text[i : i+len(q)]
                if exact && part == q || !exact && strings.EqualFold(part, q) {
                        out = append(out, diff.Span{Start: i, End: i + len(q)})
                        i += len(q)
                        continue
                }
                _, n := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(text[i:])
                i += n
        }
        return out
}

// hit is a line found by /: side's line of diff row row.
type hit struct {
        row  int
        side side
}

// order is where h stands among the lines of its diff: by row, the left
// line first.
func (h hit) order() int { return 2*h.row + int(h.side) }

// hits returns the lines of d that m finds something in, in order: only
// the changed ones unless all is set.
func (d *fileDiff) hits(m *matcher, all bool) []hit {
        var out []hit
        for i, r := range d.rows {
                if !all && r.Kind == diff.Equal {
                        continue
                }
                for _, s := range []side{left, right} {
                        if line := lineOf(r, s); line >= 0 && m.spans(d.lines[s][line]) != nil {
                                out = append(out, hit{i, s})
                        }
                }
        }
        return out
}

// nextHit returns the index of the hit to go to in direction dir among n
// hits in order, which compare compares to the cursor: forward the first
// after it, backward the last before it, so that the cursor's own line is
// passed; false past the last or before the first, as the search does not
// wrap around.
func nextHit(n, dir int, compare func(i int) int) (int, bool) {
        before := func(i int) bool {
                if dir > 0 {
                        return compare(i) <= 0
                }
                return compare(i) < 0
        }
        after := 0 // the first hit not before the cursor
        for after < n && before(after) {
                after++
        }
        if dir > 0 {
                return after, after < n
        }
        return after - 1, after > 0
}

// showMatch returns the horizontal scroll that shows the first match of m
// in the line of side s of d, in a view viewWidth columns wide, scrolled by
// xoff: xoff when lines are wrapped or the match is in view, else in steps of
// 8 columns, as h and l scroll, the match a step from the left edge.
func (d *fileDiff) showMatch(m *matcher, s side, line, xoff, viewWidth int) int {
        spans := m.spans(d.lines[s][line])
        if d.cfg.Settings.Wrap || len(spans) == 0 {
                return xoff
        }
        width := d.contentWidth(viewWidth, !d.cfg.Settings.SideBySide)
        text := d.lines[s][line]
        from := ansi.StringWidth(printable(text[:spans[0].Start]))
        to := from + ansi.StringWidth(printable(text[spans[0].Start:spans[0].End]))
        if from >= xoff && to <= xoff+width-1 { // the last column may hold »
                return xoff
        }
        return max(from-8, 0) / 8 * 8
}

// nothingSearched is the message of . and , before the first search.
const nothingSearched = "nothing searched yet: / searches"

// searchMsg returns the status message of match i of n for q.
func searchMsg(q config.Query, i, n int) string {
        return queryName(q) + fmt.Sprintf(": match %d/%d · . next · , previous", i+1, n)
}

// queryName returns q as the status line names it.
func queryName(q config.Query) string {
        if q.Regex {
                return "regex /" + q.Text
        }
        return "/" + q.Text
}

// noFurther returns the status message when no match of q lies further in
// direction dir: the cursor stays.
func noFurther(q config.Query, dir int, where string) string {
        return queryName(q) + ": " + ends(dir > 0, "no match further down", "no match further up") + strings.Replace(where, " of ", " in ", 1)
}

// scope returns where / searches, as messages name it.
func scope(st settings.Settings) string {
        if st.SearchAll {
                return "all lines"
        }
        return "changed lines"
}

// findText moves the cursor to the next line, in direction dir, that holds
// what config.Search searched for, past the cursor's own line; past the
// last or first match it stays. Without wrapping lines, it scrolls the
// match into view.
func (m *Model) findText(dir int) {
        q := m.cfg.Search.Query
        mt := matcherOf(q)
        if mt == nil {
                m.msg = nothingSearched
                return
        }
        hits := m.hits(mt, m.cfg.Settings.SearchAll)
        if len(hits) == 0 {
                m.msg = "no match for " + q.Text + " in " + scope(m.cfg.Settings)
                return
        }
        m.syncLayout()
        pos := hit{0, m.active}
        if m.cursor < len(m.view) {
                pos.row = m.view[m.cursor].row
        }
        i, ok := nextHit(len(hits), dir, func(i int) int { return cmp.Compare(hits[i].order(), pos.order()) })
        if !ok {
                m.msg = noFurther(q, dir, "")
                return
        }
        h := hits[i]
        line := lineOf(m.rows[h.row], h.side)
        m.jumpTo(m.reveal(h.side, line, m.shape()))
        m.xoff = m.showMatch(mt, h.side, line, m.xoff, m.width)
        m.msg = searchMsg(q, i, len(hits))
}

// flowHit is a line found by / in the flow view: hit in file of part.
type flowHit struct {
        part, file int
        hit
}

// findText moves the cursor to the next line, in direction dir, that holds
// what config.Search searched for, past the cursor's own line, in all
// files; in the log in those of the cursor's commit, N and P go to the
// others. Past the last or first match it stays. Without wrapping lines, it
// scrolls the match into view.
func (f *Flow) findText(dir int) {
        q := f.cfg.Search.Query
        mt := matcherOf(q)
        if mt == nil {
                f.msg = nothingSearched
                return
        }
        cur := 0 // the cursor's part
        if f.cursor < len(f.rows) {
                cur = f.rows[f.cursor].part
        }
        where := ""
        if f.inLog() {
                where = " of this commit"
        }
        var hits []flowHit
        for p, part := range f.parts {
                if part.app == nil || f.inLog() && p != cur {
                        continue
                }
                for i, e := range part.app.files {
                        if e.Binary {
                                continue
                        }
                        for _, h := range part.app.diffOf(e).hits(mt, f.cfg.Settings.SearchAll) {
                                hits = append(hits, flowHit{p, i, h})
                        }
                }
        }
        if len(hits) == 0 {
                f.msg = "no match for " + q.Text + " in " + scope(f.cfg.Settings) + where
                return
        }
        // The cursor's position: a commit's or file's own rows come before the
        // lines of its files.
        pos := flowHit{hit: hit{-1, f.active}}
        if f.cursor < len(f.rows) {
                r := f.rows[f.cursor]
                pos.part, pos.file = r.part, r.file
                if r.kind == flowDiff {
                        pos.row = f.parts[r.part].app.files[r.file].diff.view[r.line].row
                }
        }
        i, ok := nextHit(len(hits), dir, func(i int) int {
                a := hits[i]
                return cmp.Or(cmp.Compare(a.part, pos.part), cmp.Compare(a.file, pos.file), cmp.Compare(a.order(), pos.order()))
        })
        if !ok {
                f.msg = noFurther(q, dir, where)
                return
        }
        h := hits[i]
        d := f.parts[h.part].app.files[h.file].diff
        line := lineOf(d.rows[h.row], h.side)
        at := d.reveal(h.side, line, f.built)
        f.relocate() // a fold may have opened: the cursor keeps its row
        f.jumpTo(f.findDiff(h.part, h.file, at.v, at.sub))
        f.active, f.msg = h.side, searchMsg(q, i, len(hits))
        f.xoff = d.showMatch(mt, h.side, line, f.xoff, f.width)
}

// SearchBar wraps the view: / opens a bar at the bottom to type what to
// search for, in changed lines or, switched with tab, in all, plain text
// or, switched with shift+tab, a regular expression; both are toggles,
// kept in state.toml. Up and down go through what was searched for
// before, enter searches from the cursor on, as . does. What was searched
// for is kept in config.Search.
type SearchBar struct {
        tea.Model
        cfg   *config.Config
        input *lineInput // while the bar is open
        regex bool       // the text is a regular expression, as last searched or toggled
        // hist is the entry of config.Search.History shown, its length for the
        // text typed, draft, kept while going through the history.
        hist  int
        draft config.Query
        err   string // why the regular expression does not compile, or the state is not saved

        width, height int
}

// WithSearch returns m with the search bar.
func WithSearch(cfg *config.Config, m tea.Model) *SearchBar { return &SearchBar{Model: m, cfg: cfg} }

func (s *SearchBar) Update(msg tea.Msg) (tea.Model, tea.Cmd) {
        switch msg := msg.(type) {
        case tea.WindowSizeMsg:
                s.width, s.height = msg.Width, msg.Height
        case askSearchMsg:
                s.input, s.regex, s.err = &lineInput{}, s.cfg.Settings.SearchRegex, ""
                s.hist = len(s.cfg.Search.History)
                return s, nil
        case tea.KeyPressMsg:
                if s.input != nil {
                        return s, s.key(msg)
                }
        case tea.PasteMsg:
                if s.input != nil {
                        s.err = ""
                        s.input.text += msg.Content
                        return s, nil
                }
        }
        var cmd tea.Cmd
        s.Model, cmd = s.Model.Update(msg)
        return s, cmd
}

// key edits the query: enter searches it, esc, enter on nothing typed or
// backspace on it closes the bar; tab switches between changed and all
// lines, shift+tab between plain text and regular expression; up and
// down go through the history, each query in its mode.
func (s *SearchBar) key(k tea.KeyPressMsg) tea.Cmd {
        in, history, st := s.input, s.cfg.Search.History, &s.cfg.Settings
        s.err = ""
        show := func(q config.Query) { in.text, s.regex = q.Text, q.Regex }
        switch {
        case k.String() == "ctrl+c":
                return tea.Quit
        case in.cancels(k):
                s.input = nil
                return nil
        }
        switch k.String() {
        case "enter":
                q := config.Query{Text: in.text, Regex: s.regex}
                if q.Text == "" {
                        s.input = nil
                        return nil
                }
                if _, err := compile(q); err != nil {
                        s.err = err.Error()
                        return nil
                }
                s.input = nil
                s.cfg.Search.Add(q)
                if st.SearchRegex != q.Regex { // from the history
                        st.SearchRegex = q.Regex
                        s.err = saveState(s.cfg)
                }
                return func() tea.Msg { return findMsg(1) }
        case "tab":
                s.err = toggle(s.cfg, &st.SearchAll)
        case "shift+tab":
                s.regex = !s.regex
                st.SearchRegex = s.regex
                s.err = saveState(s.cfg)
        case "up":
                if s.hist == len(history) {
                        s.draft = config.Query{Text: in.text, Regex: s.regex}
                }
                if s.hist > 0 {
                        s.hist--
                        show(history[s.hist])
                }
        case "down":
                if s.hist < len(history) {
                        s.hist++
                        if s.hist == len(history) {
                                show(s.draft)
                        } else {
                                show(history[s.hist])
                        }
                }
        default:
                in.edit(k)
        }
        return nil
}

// frame draws the bar over the view, above its status line, while it is
// open: the query, and why its regular expression does not compile, see
// inputBox.
func (s *SearchBar) View() tea.View { return tea.NewView(s.frame()) }

func (s *SearchBar) frame() string {
        base := s.Model.View().Content
        if s.input == nil || s.width == 0 {
                return base
        }
        t := s.cfg.Theme
        // The title says how the bar searches and what tab and shift+tab switch
        // to.
        mode, otherMode := "search", "regex"
        if s.regex {
                mode, otherMode = "regex", "text"
        }
        otherScope := "all"
        if s.cfg.Settings.SearchAll {
                otherScope = "changed"
        }
        keys := []string{"enter find", "tab " + otherScope, "shift+tab " + otherMode, "esc cancel"}
        if len(s.cfg.Search.History) > 0 {
                keys = slices.Insert(keys, 1, "↑↓ history")
        }
        title := popupTitle(t, mode+" "+scope(s.cfg.Settings), "", strings.Join(keys, " · "))
        bar, w := inputBox(t, s.width, title, "/"+printable(s.input.text), s.err, false)
        // Above the status line, the last row.
        y := max(strings.Count(base, "\n")-strings.Count(bar, "\n"), 0)
        return overlayAt(base, bar, max((s.width-w)/2, 0), y, w, s.width)
}

package ui

import (
        "cmp"
        _ "embed"
        "errors"
        "fmt"
        "io/fs"
        "os"
        "path/filepath"
        "regexp"
        "slices"
        "strconv"
        "strings"

        tea "charm.land/bubbletea/v2"
        "github.com/BurntSushi/toml"
        "github.com/charmbracelet/x/ansi"

        "jdiff/internal/config"
)

// settingOption is a line of the settings popup: the key that toggles it
// there and on the pages, the action of its row in keybindings.toml, what it
// does while on, from that row, and the toggle. With ask, the page below
// toggles it, when it can. With logFlow, its key works only in the log's flow
// view, where it shows; in the popup always. With number instead of the toggle,
// it is a number, asked for in a line; it has no key, the cursor goes to it in
// the popup.
type settingOption struct {
        key, action, what string
        setting           func(*config.Config) *bool
        ask               tea.Cmd
        logFlow           bool
        number            func(*config.Config) *int
}

var settingOptions = settingWords([]settingOption{
        {action: "layout", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.SideBySide }},
        {action: "flow", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.Flow }, ask: toggleFlow},
        {action: "untracked", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.Untracked }, ask: toggleUntracked},
        {action: "wrap", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.Wrap }},
        {action: "whitespace", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.ShowWhitespaces }},
        {action: "mark_whitespace", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.MarkWhitespace }},
        {action: "trailing", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.ShowTrailing }},
        {action: "line_ends", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.ShowCR }},
        {action: "bold", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.BoldChanges }},
        {action: "preview", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.Preview }},
        {action: "full_messages", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.FullMessages }, logFlow: true},
        {action: "stay_in_commit", setting: func(c *config.Config) *bool { return &c.Settings.StayInCommit }, logFlow: true},
        {action: "auto_reload_timeout", number: func(c *config.Config) *int { return &c.Settings.AutoReloadTimeout }},
})

// settingWords returns options with their keys and what they do from
// keybindings.toml.
func settingWords(options []settingOption) []settingOption {
        for i := range options {
                r := keyFile.row(options[i].action)
                if len(r.Keys) > 0 {
                        options[i].key = r.Keys[0]
                }
                options[i].what = keyFile.keyText(r.Setting)
        }
        return options
}

// keybindingsFile binds the keys of all pages to actions, gives their words and
// decides the order of the settings.
//
//go:embed keybindings.toml
var keybindingsFile string

// keyRow is a row of keybindings.toml.
type keyRow struct {
        Action  string
        Keys    []string
        Label   []string
        Help    string
        Setting string
}

// helpRow is a line of the help popup in keybindings.toml.
type helpRow struct{ Heading, Note, Action string }

// keyPages holds keybindings.toml: the rows of each page and the help's lines.
type keyPages struct {
        CommitList []keyRow  `toml:"commit_list"`
        FileList   []keyRow  `toml:"file_list"`
        DiffView   []keyRow  `toml:"diff_view"`
        FlowView   []keyRow  `toml:"flow_view"`
        Everywhere []keyRow  `toml:"everywhere"`
        DocView    []keyRow  `toml:"doc_view"`
        Help       []helpRow `toml:"help"`
}

func readKeys(text string) (keyPages, error) {
        var p keyPages
        _, err := toml.Decode(text, &p)
        return p, err
}

// keyFile is keybindingsFile, read. It is part of the program, so that failing
// to read it is a bug, which TestKeybindingsFile finds.
var keyFile = func() keyPages {
        p, err := readKeys(keybindingsFile)
        if err != nil {
                panic(err)
        }
        return p
}()

// row returns the row that gives the keys and words of action; see
// keybindings.toml for the order. Without one, it has no keys.
func (p keyPages) row(action string) keyRow {
        if rows := p.rowsOf(action); len(rows) > 0 {
                return rows[0]
        }
        return keyRow{}
}

// rowsOf returns the rows of action in the first page that has it, in the
// order of row.
func (p keyPages) rowsOf(action string) []keyRow {
        for _, rows := range [][]keyRow{p.Everywhere, p.DiffView, p.FlowView, p.CommitList, p.FileList, p.DocView} {
                var of []keyRow
                for _, r := range rows {
                        if r.Action == action {
                                of = append(of, r)
                        }
                }
                if len(of) > 0 {
                        return of
                }
        }
        return nil
}

// placeholder matches {action} and {action:n} in the words of keybindings.toml.
var placeholder = regexp.MustCompile(`\{([a-z_]+)(?::([0-9]+))?\}`)

// keyText returns s with each placeholder replaced by the keys of its row, ""
// for a row that does not exist.
func (p keyPages) keyText(s string) string {
        return placeholder.ReplaceAllStringFunc(s, func(m string) string {
                sub := placeholder.FindStringSubmatch(m)
                n, _ := strconv.Atoi(sub[2])
                if rows := p.rowsOf(sub[1]); n < len(rows) {
                        return strings.Join(rows[n].Keys, " ")
                }
                return ""
        })
}

// twoWay lists the actions whose rows take two keys, one for each way.
var twoWay = []string{"move", "page", "half_page", "ends", "scroll", "change", "next_file", "history", "match", "link", "line_link", "next_page"}

// userRow is a row of the user's keybindings.toml, which changes only keys.
type userRow struct {
        Action string
        Keys   []string
}

// userKeys returns p with the keys of text, the user's keybindings.toml, and
// what is wrong with it. The rows of an action on a page replace all its rows
// there, keeping their words; a file with a problem changes nothing, so that
// a mistake cannot take away the keys to quit or get help.
func (p keyPages) userKeys(text string) (keyPages, []string) {
        var u struct {
                CommitList []userRow `toml:"commit_list"`
                FileList   []userRow `toml:"file_list"`
                DiffView   []userRow `toml:"diff_view"`
                FlowView   []userRow `toml:"flow_view"`
                Everywhere []userRow `toml:"everywhere"`
                DocView    []userRow `toml:"doc_view"`
        }
        md, err := toml.Decode(text, &u)
        if err != nil {
                return p, []string{err.Error()}
        }
        var problems []string
        for _, k := range md.Undecoded() {
                problems = append(problems, "unknown "+k.String())
        }
        merged := p
        for _, t := range []struct {
                name string
                rows *[]keyRow
                user []userRow
        }{
                {"everywhere", &merged.Everywhere, u.Everywhere}, {"diff_view", &merged.DiffView, u.DiffView},
                {"flow_view", &merged.FlowView, u.FlowView}, {"commit_list", &merged.CommitList, u.CommitList},
                {"file_list", &merged.FileList, u.FileList}, {"doc_view", &merged.DocView, u.DocView},
        } {
                for _, r := range t.user {
                        switch {
                        case !slices.ContainsFunc(*t.rows, func(b keyRow) bool { return b.Action == r.Action }):
                                problems = append(problems, "no action "+r.Action+" in "+t.name)
                        case slices.Contains(twoWay, r.Action) && len(r.Keys) != 2:
                                problems = append(problems, r.Action+" in "+t.name+" takes two keys, one each way")
                        }
                }
                *t.rows = replaceKeys(*t.rows, t.user)
        }
        problems = append(problems, merged.conflicts()...)
        if len(problems) > 0 {
                return p, problems
        }
        return merged, nil
}

// replaceKeys returns rows with the rows of each action of user in place of
// its rows: the first where the first was, with the words of the row it
// replaces; rows of user beyond them have none.
func replaceKeys(rows []keyRow, user []userRow) []keyRow {
        var out []keyRow
        done := map[string]bool{}
        for _, r := range rows {
                if !slices.ContainsFunc(user, func(u userRow) bool { return u.Action == r.Action }) {
                        out = append(out, r)
                        continue
                }
                if done[r.Action] {
                        continue
                }
                done[r.Action] = true
                var old []keyRow
                for _, o := range rows {
                        if o.Action == r.Action {
                                old = append(old, o)
                        }
                }
                i := 0
                for _, u := range user {
                        if u.Action != r.Action {
                                continue
                        }
                        row := keyRow{Action: u.Action}
                        if i < len(old) {
                                row = old[i]
                        }
                        row.Keys = u.Keys
                        out = append(out, row)
                        i++
                }
        }
        return out
}

// KeysFile is the user's keybindings.toml in the configuration directory.
const KeysFile = "keybindings.toml"

// LoadKeys reads the user's keybindings.toml from the configuration directory
// of cfg, if there is one, over the built-in keys; a problem with it is a
// warning of cfg, and the built-in keys stay. Call it before the first page is
// made, which shows the warnings, and before OrderSettings.
func LoadKeys(cfg *config.Config) {
        if cfg.Dir() == "" {
                return
        }
        data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(cfg.Dir(), KeysFile))
        if errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist) {
                return
        }
        p, problems := keyFile, []string(nil)
        if err != nil {
                problems = []string{err.Error()}
        } else {
                p, problems = keyFile.userKeys(string(data))
        }
        if len(problems) > 0 {
                cfg.AddWarning(KeysFile + ": " + strings.Join(problems, "; ") + "; the built-in keys are used")
                return
        }
        keyFile = p
        settingOptions = settingWords(settingOptions)
        bindings = keyFile.bindings()
}

// keyPage is a page whose keys are looked up in keybindings.toml.
type keyPage int

const (
        commitList keyPage = iota
        fileList
        diffView
        flowView
        docView
        everywhere // the popups over all pages
)

// keyHit is what a key does: the action of its row, "" for none, and its place
// in the row, which gives the way of actions that go two ways (see
// keybindings.toml).
type keyHit struct {
        action string
        i      int
}

// tables returns the rows the keys of page pg are looked up in, in order.
func (p keyPages) tables(pg keyPage) [][]keyRow {
        switch pg {
        case commitList:
                return [][]keyRow{p.CommitList, p.Everywhere}
        case fileList:
                return [][]keyRow{p.FileList, p.Everywhere}
        case diffView:
                return [][]keyRow{p.DiffView, p.Everywhere}
        case flowView:
                return [][]keyRow{p.FlowView, p.DiffView, p.Everywhere}
        case docView:
                return [][]keyRow{p.DocView, p.Everywhere}
        }
        return [][]keyRow{p.Everywhere}
}

// key returns what key k, as Bubble Tea names it, does on page pg.
func (p keyPages) key(pg keyPage, k string) keyHit {
        for _, rows := range p.tables(pg) {
                for _, r := range rows {
                        for i, name := range r.Keys {
                                if keyName(name) == k {
                                        return keyHit{r.Action, i}
                                }
                        }
                }
        }
        return keyHit{}
}

// isAction reports whether key k does action on all pages: the popups over
// them open and close on such keys.
func isAction(k, action string) bool { return keyFile.key(everywhere, k).action == action }

// keyName returns Bubble Tea's name of the key written as name in
// keybindings.toml, which writes pgdn and F5 as the help shows them, and
// <letter> for the letter that follows.
func keyName(name string) string {
        name = strings.TrimSuffix(name, "<letter>")
        if name == "pgdn" {
                return "pgdown"
        }
        if n, ok := strings.CutPrefix(name, "F"); ok && n != "" && strings.Trim(n, "0123456789") == "" {
                return "f" + n
        }
        return name
}

// label returns the word of the status bar in state, "" when there is none.
func (r keyRow) label(state int) string {
        if state < len(r.Label) {
                return r.Label[state]
        }
        return ""
}

// order returns the first key of each row of everywhere, which decides the
// order of the settings.
func (p keyPages) order() []string {
        var keys []string
        for _, r := range p.Everywhere {
                if len(r.Keys) > 0 {
                        keys = append(keys, r.Keys[0])
                }
        }
        return keys
}

// orderSettings returns options and bindings with the settings in the order of
// keys. Options not in keys, as c and C, which keybindings.toml lists with the
// flow view, stay behind, as they are; the other bindings stay where they are.
func orderSettings(keys []string, options []settingOption, bs []binding) ([]settingOption, []binding) {
        rank := func(k string) int {
                if i := slices.Index(keys, k); i >= 0 {
                        return i
                }
                return len(keys)
        }
        options = slices.Clone(options)
        slices.SortStableFunc(options, func(a, b settingOption) int { return cmp.Compare(rank(a.key), rank(b.key)) })

        isSetting := func(b binding) bool {
                return b.key != "" && slices.Contains(keys, b.key) && slices.ContainsFunc(options, func(o settingOption) bool { return o.key == b.key })
        }
        bs = slices.Clone(bs)
        var at []int
        var settings []binding
        for i, b := range bs {
                if isSetting(b) {
                        at, settings = append(at, i), append(settings, b)
                }
        }
        slices.SortStableFunc(settings, func(a, b binding) int { return cmp.Compare(rank(a.key), rank(b.key)) })
        for i, b := range settings {
                bs[at[i]] = b
        }
        return options, bs
}

// OrderSettings puts the settings of the popup and the help in the order of
// keybindings.toml. Call it before the first view is drawn.
func OrderSettings() {
        settingOptions, bindings = orderSettings(keyFile.order(), settingOptions, bindings)
}

// keyOrderProblems returns what is wrong with text, a keybindings.toml: a row of
// all pages with a key that does not exist or stands twice, a setting missing or
// without words, a line of the help with an action that has no help, a key with
// two actions on one page, a placeholder without its row.
